The elder's sap-buds cost it 0.1 to 0.2 deep notches against the same elder with none. **Shortfalls:** D26 quoted 2.6 deep notches a frame at `mood` −1 for SW's 4-quill back, and the built comb gives 1.5; the young's deep notches at −1 are 0.5, because VR7 pins its 11 px peak. **The v1 figures:** the current rig gives 4.7 | 0.7, 4.1 | 0.1 and 2.4 | 1.4 for the v1 adult's idle, `mood` −1 and asleep, where the list above SW's once gave 4.7 | 1.1, 4.3 | 0.0 and 1.1 | 0.6; those asleep figures probably predate the level crown, and two independent counters agree on the current rig (the v1 comb was not re-run in the element pass). The mood gauge keeps its fractions (all heights scale together: 69 % of upright, 78 % of rest at −1). The taller mid-back quills show over the spread leaf through the preen's hold, the round-2 gap.
- **Along the tail.** The quills carry on along the *top* of the tail, shrinking toward the tip. The tail tip itself is a plain taper (D5). A tail quill stands only while its side of the tail faces at least 37° above level, growing in to full by 53°: at 14°, the first quill of a tail hanging from the rump (the young's quill ball, the lie-down, the wake) stuck straight back out as a lone stinger.
- **Lean and length, driven by `mood` like every other cue (D7).** `mood` −1: the quills **shrink to × 0.8** (bases too) and lean only **30°** back, a sad, flattened comb that keeps 0.8 cos 30° = 69 % of the upright height; `mood` 0: 28° at full length; `mood` +1: 15°, perky, with the ripple. Babies lean only (25° → 14° → 7°). Leaning the droop out instead (50°; the young 36°), each tip overhung the notch behind it by h sin(lean), 9.2 and 5.3 px against quills 5.5 and 7.3 px apart, and at ÷ 3 phase 2 the young's back was flat with one step and the adult's a flat-topped box (the cast reviews, rounds 1 and 2). At 30° the overhang is 0.4 h: counted on the ÷ 3 top edge at phase 2, 3 or more notches on 8 of 9 sampled frames of the young (seeds 1–3, t 0 / 40 / 80; 2 on the ninth) and 9 of 9 of the adult. At × 0.74 (the gauge's 64 %) the young's teeth stood 4 px and phase 2 kept two. (The rest lean was 35°; at /3 the young saw came down to 2 px bumps.) Hungry and sad read as a droop, happy as a lift, the same direction as the other five cues.
  - The **back** quills' lean is measured from the **world's** upright (the body's pitch leaned back out), capped relative to the body where lean and length together keep 64 % of the upright height (50° at full length, 36° at −1's × 0.8; babies 25°); the tail quills lean from their own tail segment. Measured from the body, the beg's 14° nose-up sit made its 39–47° droop stick out flat behind like twigs. Bristle and the breath's wind-up bring them upright **and** full length. **The mark floor** (5.2): a quill's base is never under 4 px after the mood's × 0.8 (`BASE_MIN`), and a tail quill is never under 5 px after the −1 variant (`TAIL_MIN`): a −1 pet at `mood` −1 drew the last adult tail quill 3.2 on 3.2 px; it is now 4 on 4.
  - A drooping quill is a **thorn**: its tail-side base corner runs back with the tip by up to half the tip's lean (h sin(lean) / 2), eased in from 30° and full at 50°. Sheared on a fixed base, a quill at 39–50° showed 1–2 px of bone between two ink edges and the comb read as hatching; widened at the 28° rest too, the /3 saw filled into a smooth hump.
- **Bristle (a separate alarm one-shot, the `bristle` channel).** Triggered by a stroke on the back, a loud noise or a shriek nearby: the quills snap upright (0°) and grow × 1.15, with a 1 px tremble every 4 f, for 30 f, then ease back to the mood lean. It is the only state where the quills stand fully upright, so alarm never looks like happiness.

| Feature | Baby | Young | Adult | Elder (weathered) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Quills (count × height, base; v2 spikier, D26: back `[x rump 0 → neck 1, h, base]`, tail `[t, h, base]`) | 3 soft nubs, points rounded 1 px, on the **loin, rump and tail**: rump `[−8.75, 8, 5]`, loin `[−2.25, 7, 5]`, tail `[0.6, 6, 5]` (the rump nub at −8.25 left 2 of 27 frames with no ÷ 3 notch, seed 2 idle phase 0 and `mood` −1 phase 1; at −8.75 every frame keeps one and the deep notches rise, idle 0.3 → 0.6 and `mood` −1 0.4 → 0.6: the element pass v2; v1: 6, 6 and 5 tall at −7.75 / −2; SW's 10 / 9 / 8 stood 67 % of the adult and put a 10 px lump behind the hips beside the baby's 5 × 7 flame, fire's zone: the v2 review). Taller nubs, not more: a 4th nub took the deep notches from 0.2 to 0 a frame; the baby's head covers the front 60 % of its back. Lean at half the adult range (25° → 14° → 7°). **Asleep** `[8, 7]` + `[6]` | 3 on the back `[0.04, 8, 6] [0.48, 11, 7] [0.92, 9, 6]` (v1 [8, 10, 7]; SW's 13 px peak was 87 % of the adult, a sprout no longer) + **2 on the tail** `[0.20, 7, 5] [0.42, 5, 5]` (v1: one): the added tail quill is the young's growth step. Three at about 7 px keep a saw at ÷ 3 even at `mood` −1 (four teeth over the young's short back merged into a flat edge). **Asleep** `[9, 8, 7]` + `[6, 5]`, graded against the back line so the tips run level | **4** on the back `[0, 10, 8] [0.333, 14, 8] [0.667, 15, 8] [1.0, 10, 8]` (v1: 5, [7, 11, 12, 10, 7]) + **4 on the tail** at t 0.14 / 0.32 / 0.50 / 0.68, heights 9 / 7 / 6 / 5, bases 5 / 5 / 5 / 4 (≤ the 6.8 px pitch − 1; v1: 3, [6, 5, 4]); none past t 0.68, so the last ~12 px of the tail are a plain taper. **Asleep** tall and short by turns, `[13, 5, 13, 12]` + `[9, 7, 6, 5]`: a level crown. Wide back bases (at 5 to 6 px a leaning quill left 1 to 2 px of bone between two ink edges, a grey scribble; the tail's shorter quills stand closer to their own segment). All in plain `horn`, no dark tips | **the adult's comb, unchanged**: no quill chipped, taller or worn (wear is the wings' alone, D21), plus the **sap-buds** (below). **Asleep** as the adult |
| Brow thorns: 2, pointing back, lying back along the neck line, rooted on the cranium's **back** contour (145°) as fire's horns are (rooted on its top, at 105°, they stood up like ears; then, lying back, they lay along the skull's own top and read as a pale strap or goggles), tapering to a point (r → 0.5: with a round 2 px end the thorn read at game scale as a pale capsule, a hair clip or a bandage), and never more than 20° above or 40° below straight back in the world (3.0) | none | 3 px, r 1.5 → 0.5 (the far one culled: one thorn) | **7 px** (v2, built; 7 + the seeded +1 = 8, the adult head cap), r 1.5 → 0.5; the far thorn forks at 8° (`farTilt`), so the overlap cull removes it and one thorn shows, as on the young (at the adult default 28°, and still at 16–20°, it lay over the near one as a flat grey slab, a hair clip) | 7 px, as the adult |
| Wings: leaf-shaped, smooth convex trailing edge, the spar is the leaf's vein | nub | 2 spars | 3 spars. Each spar pokes 3 px past the membrane as a thorn, plus a 4 px wrist thorn. | 3 spars with the thorns, as the adult (parts.ts must let the elder through its adult-only thorn test); a round bite 5 × 3 in the leaf's edge and a notched 4 × 3 hole against the forearm at full spread (2.9) |
| `foldRise` | 0: the nub rests at 200°, along the flank below the quills, so it never hides one | 0: a folded wing never hides a quill | 0 | 0 |
| Markings: dark tail rings, 3 px wide; the first at the tail base at every stage, just behind the hip where the tail shows (15 % of a young or adult tail, 32 % of the baby's short one), later rings further out | 1 (tail base) | 2 (+ 45 %) | 3 (+ 40 %, 62 %) | 3, as the adult |

- **Adult-only extra:** the wing thorns.
- **Elder (v2).** The adult's comb, unworn (the table). Spike's body greys only as an elder, to a lichen green (scale weight 0.9, lightening a little, 3.9), its belly and leaf at 0.6 (fully they went olive-drab), its bone quills never greying, so the pale comb gains contrast as the body greys; its adult grey is the silver back. With the elder's head carried only 3 to 5 px lower (2.1), the tallest quills stay under the head's top, a comb and not a threat display. **Elder-only extra: sap-buds**, drawn at every mood. Two **round** buds, 4 × 4 with their corners cut, flat and inked: lit `glow` over a 1-row `glow.sh` `#53b075` calyx at the base, so each reads as a bud on its stem (56 % apart), all `glow.sh` banked at `mood` ≤ −0.3 and asleep (cast review v2: the first build's 3 × 3 `glow.sh` squares with a 2 × 2 glint at `mood` ≥ 0.5 read as square green rivets or LEDs; a 3 × 3 with its corners cut would read as a "+"), each **nestled** at the root of a notch, its ring against the quills' roots (the bud and the bone are 58 % apart):
  - one in the notch **behind the rump quill** (between it and the first tail quill), on the dorsal line (the rump's top or the tail root's, whichever is higher), 3 px behind the rump quill's tail-side root corner;
  - one on the tail between its first two quills, 1.5 px toward the tip from their midpoint, its centre 0.5 px inside the tail's top.

  A notch is narrower than a bud until about 6 px up, so "≥ 1 px clear of any quill's ink" cannot hold: a bud raised to clear it sat on a quill's bone in mid-air and filled the notch at ÷ 3. **Why behind the rump quill:** in front of it the back bud lay under the folded near wing, and only 2 to 4 of its 9 px showed from t 45 to 120 of the idle and on each walk stride (a whole-pixel stamp against a sub-pixel edge, 5.1 #12). Behind it all 9 px show on every frame of the idle and its variants, the walk, sleep, beg, eat, fidget, pet and breath, seeds 1 to 3, at every mood; only a spread near wing covers it, and holds still while it does (the happy's preen, 2 of 9 px; the wake's raised stretch, all of it for about 16 f). The tail bud seated 1.5 px out filled its notch at `mood` −1 (this, not the back bud, cost the elder a ÷ 3 notch); it hides only where that side of the tail faces the floor. The two read as a pair at the hip, about 8 px apart on either side of the first tail quill. At `mood` ≥ 0.5 each carries a 2 × 2 glint in `glow` (56 % over the bud: a `glow.hi` glint on a `glow` bud was 5 %, invisible). "The old bramble fruits."
- **Signature: Quill Volley.**
  - **Wind-up:** the quills snap upright over 4 f, and the body puffs (stretch 1.06).
  - **Volley:** one real quill per back quill, the adult's **4** (v2; v1's 5), the young's 3, fly out in a forward-up fan: all launched from the neck base within 4 f, the most forward quill first, from 20° above level to 60°, toward the facing side at 3 px/f, so the fan opens in front of and above the head (launched straight up first, 85° → 40° 2 f apart, the five stood in one column behind the head and read as a crest or a feather duster). They are drawn with the back row, before the body, so the neck and head lie over them until they clear the silhouette. Each is `horn`, 3 × 6, ink-outlined, trailing a 6 px sap streak (`glow`, flat, with a 1 px `scale` edge so it reads on the pale floor; at α 0.5 it was 5 % from the floor), the streak in whole pixels.
  - **Leaving the comb:** back quill k fires as volley quill n − 1 − k and leaves the comb 5 f after its launch, as its flight comes out from behind the neck and head, front first, one a frame. Gone at the snap, the whole comb vanished while the flights were still hidden: a bare back with nothing in the air. (Launched each from its own place on the back, the quills flew as a parallel row over the back and head: a crest again.)
  - **After 20 f:** each quill pops into **one** sap sparkle for 6 f (2 × 2 `glow.hi` with a 1 px `scale` ring on its four sides, corners cut: a full 4 × 4 box round a pale centre read as a hollow square, and two per quill read as pairs of rings, "oo oo oo"). The fired quills regrow from nothing over 30 f, each base shrinking with its height (full from ⅔ grown, never under 4 px), and a quill under 3 px tall is not drawn: at full base the first frames were ink crumbs along the back line.
  - **Baby:** it bristles into a puffball (squash 1.15 wide). At the snap the **rump** nub pops off (v2: drawn 5 × 8 to match the taller rump nub) with one sap sparkle, thrown ≥ 14 px up and 8 px over the head, tumbling a quarter turn every 4 f (its bottom 2 px in `horn`'s shadow tone: all cream, on the straw only its ink read, a hollow "▷" play glyph), while the head tips back to watch it ("ah-", cue 4–10). At cue 12 it lands on the back of the head: BONK, the sneeze ("CHOO": the head snaps 10° down), `dazed`, and 3 sap drops fanning out of the snout at −40 / 0 / +30° and 2 / 2.4 / 1.6 px/f for 8 f, born 3 px clear of it (the level one fastest, so they spread as a ">" burst). The nub hops 3 px off the head and drops back into its own socket at cue 18, upright: no crumb, no regrow. (Thrown up and back it hung over the tail nub, then lay behind the tail away from the face; landed on the crown it sat among the dazed stars.)
- **Ambient:**
  - Quill ripple: each quill (the tail quills included) leans 15° toward upright in turn, from rump to neck, 4 f apart, 10 f each, never more upright than 4°, only at `mood` ≥ 0; babies at 0.6× and 1 px taller at the peak. Each wave comes 240 ± 60 f after the last (each jittered ± 30 f about its 240 f slot: independent ± 60 jitter gave gaps of 120 to 360 f), 1.5× slower with more than 4 dragons on screen.
  - Idle fidget: it grooms its quills (44 f; young × 0.85, baby × 0.6). The head eases round (`inout`; keyed −150, with the neck's arch the whole head angle reaches about −195 to −220) past vertical, so the rig draws it **looking back** (4.1): upright over the shoulder, snout down to the comb, the thorn on top of the skull (pitched upside down, the thorns pointed down like tusks). The eyes shut from f 3; it pecks into the comb every 4 f from f 10 to 30, working the front two quills; it eases back over f 32–44 and opens its eyes at f 40, the head within 40° of level (opening them at f 32 and whipping round 150° in 8 f showed an upside-down open eye).

### 3.4 ROCK: "Cobble", the boulder dragon
**Identity and care.**
- Calm, sleepy, stubborn and patient: **the easiest baby to raise.**
- It eats pebbles, sunbathes, naps longest and tucks under its dome like a turtle when upset.
- **Crystals grow on the dome as its bond grows** (count), and their glow shows its mood (brightness). Every rock hatches with one seed crystal, so even a new baby has a mood read.

**The cue: the boulder dome.** A faceted carapace over the back, plus a heavy, low stance (the lowest clearance in the cast).
- **Construction.** One path drawn after the body: a shell resting **on** the back. Its top edge is the body's own back line, lifted by a thickness that is the rise at the centre and tapers to 1.5 px at the ends. An elliptical arc stands its ends vertical and read as a lampshade. One convex arc (3.0), with the facet corners on it and a ridge vertex over the middle facet, so no flat lid runs across the top.
- **Rim.** It is inked, 1 px under the back line at the two ends, sagging over the flank mid-body, so it reads as a shell.
- **Facets** are tone steps with no lines: planes radiating from a point under the dome, toned **in thirds**. `marking.hi` `#a87a4e` on the lit rear facets (adult 0–1, young 0), the base colour on top (adult 2, young 1), `marking.sh` `#53423d` on the front (adult 3–4, young 2). Only the outermost facets were toned before, 3 px slivers, and the dome read as one flat brown. The baby's pebble is one tone. The elder's 6 are toned 2 / 2 / 2 (hi hi, base base, sh sh): an even count crests on the corner between its two top facets (no ridge vertex), and rolled over the elder rests on that corner.
- **Head.** Carried below the dome top, at least 2 px (young 3, adult 4.5 at rest): the neck runs out nearly level from low on the chest (2.3).

| Feature | Baby | Young | Adult | Elder (elder signature) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dome, width × rise above the back, facets | 12 × 5, 1 facet: a pebble backpack | 22 × 9, 3 facets (rise 7 → 9: with the head low it still tops the young's silhouette) | 34 × 11, 5 facets | **36 × 12, 6 facets**, grown, whole (no chip: wear is the wings' alone) |
| Crystals: `flat()` `glow` pixel constructions in face space, upright, with a 4-neighbour ink ring, on the rear third; the count is ceil(bond × n), ≥ 1 (`DragonInfo.bond`, 1 when the owner passes none) | 1 seed crystal, 3 × 4 (a 2 px crystal kept a 1 px core, under the mark floor), from hatching, at u 0.15 on the dome, where it stays ≥ 2 px clear of the baby's head (2.6) | 1 to 2: the seed grown to 3 × 5 at u 0.34, + one 3 × 5 at u 0.15 | 1 to 3: the 4 × 7 at u 0.27, + 3 × 5 at 0.11 and 0.43; one `glow.hi` facet on the 4 × 7 only (on a 3 px crystal a facet would be under 2 px), its point off-centre, stepping 4 → 2 → 1 high on the lit left. The outer crystals (the young's rear one, the adult's rear and front) lean outward 1 px per 3 rows with their points on the outer side, so the cluster fans; a dome tipped past 12° (the roll-over) leans them with it | up to 4, **max(1, ceil(bond × 3)) + 1** (2 at bond 0: always one more than an adult of the same bond, the elder-only extra, so it shows at any bond, where ceil(bond × 4) needed bond > 0.75): the elder crystal **4 × 8** at u 0.27 (the adult's bevel, its lit facet one row longer, 7 rows, 2 rows of glow under it), 3 × 5 at 0.43 and 0.08, and the outermost, **a 3 × 4 sunk one row into the crest at u 0.61**, leaning forward, its tip 2 px under the 0.43's (and 3 under the big one's). The cluster fans both ways from the big one, its tips stepping down on each side: on the crest's flat top (0.44 to 0.66), at 0.58 with all its rows, the three front tips stood level, a comb (the v2 element review). Growth order: the permanent one is the 0.43 beside the seed, so bond 0 shows a pair, not two spikes 15 px apart; the bond grows the 0.61 before the 0.08, so the cluster stays together at mid bond. Stronger shears (1 px a row) read as a fin or a flag, so the lean stays 1 px; at 9 px the cluster read as spike's saw, so it stops at 8. ÷ 3: still a dome with nubs |
| Nose horn: one, blunt, on the snout, pointing up and forward, near palette; the whole horn ≥ 2 px clear of the eye's largest box (`info.eye`). Young and adult root it on the snout's rounded top-front (its end circle, 60° up from the front) and lean it 60° up in head space: rooted at the tip and leaning forward it continued the snout's line and read as a cigar | 2 × 3 nub **on the snout tip**, leaning forward (35°): behind the tip it sat on the eye's ring | 4 px, r 2 → 1.5 | 5 px, r 3 → 1.5 | as the adult |
| Brow ridge | none | 2 px | 3 px, a contour bump in the skull path only. No lid over the eye at neutral: a permanent hood made the neutral face read as `sleepy` | 4 px |
| Tail | a stub, 0.65 as long on a thinner root (r 3.4 → 2), angled down and back, stiff: as long as the body and broad, rising up and back, it read as a beaver's tail | short and thick (root r × 1.2), leaving the hip under the dome's rim nearly level (young 14° down, adult 10°) and curving down 9° (adult 7°) a segment to rest its tip on the ground: a heavy tail with a convex top, a concave step under the rim between the dome's arc and the tail (run straight at 34° it continued the dome's back slope to the floor as one line, and the young and adult read as a wedge, a doorstop or a kickstand: the cast reviews, round 2); the chain's wobble mostly held (`tailStiff` 0.7): bent 6° a segment and let swing, the outline went wavy between segments, a limp flap; tip radius capped at 2 (≤ 4 px, water's zone rule), blunt by its round cap, no club | same | as the adult, its rest unchanged (2.3) |
| Wings: slate | a nub under the dome rim, its root 3 px further back (`rootDx` −3): at the stage root its front showed as a 3 px slate speck in the notch between shell, body and head | stubby, 0.7× span, 2 spars, 1 px scallops | stubby, 0.7×, 3 spars; flaps hard and hops 3 px. It cannot fly: its adult reward is the **boulder hop** (4.2) | stubby, 0.7×, 3 spars; one ragged 5 × 3 tear where the panel holds it, no hole (2.9) |
| Markings | the dome is the marking | same | same | same |

- **Mood.** The dome never changes, so the cue is always at 100 %.
  - `mood` ≤ −0.3: crystals drawn in `glow.sh` `#7761cf` (`moodTones().banked`), 52 % below the lit colour. They are dim, but still outlined, so the count still reads (136° of hue from the dome). The first draft's `mix(glow, scale, 0.5)` came out *lighter* than the lit crystal.
  - `mood` 0: `glow`.
  - `mood` +1: `glow`, plus a `#f8f4ec` glint travelling the cluster rear to front, 8 f on each crystal: it covers the crystal's TIP (the point and the full-width row under it; the 4 × 7 takes a 2 × 2 over its facet). A lone seed alternates its tip and a lower spot every 8 f, so mood +1 always differs from mood 0.
  - At any lit mood, a twinkle every 200 × stretch ± 60 f (one ambient particle).
  - **Two tucks that must never be confused**, because they ask for opposite responses (leave it alone, or comfort it):
    - **Sleep tuck:** the head rests on the ground *outside* the rim, snout forward, eyes `closed`; the tail wraps forward; the crystals go to `glow.sh`; "z" glyphs rise. Breath slows to 240 f.
    - **Upset tuck** (the element anim `upset`, `ACT.upset`, cue = frames into it: an intro of 16 f, young 13, baby 10, then a 96 f loop). Young and adult: the body lies flat, pitched 7° nose-down; the neck folds so the head sits back with its chin on the floor, level (2°: pitched snout-down, the upright `sad` brow read as a scowl). Tuck 1: the dome's front **rolls forward** from its crest as one convex arc, over the crown and the nose horn, down to a round lip 2 px off the floor ahead of the snout tip, a boulder; the lit top and the shaded front meet at one facet step. The rim runs back under the snout, up a wall 2 px ahead of the eye's largest box, then back over the eye, rising 6° toward the front with its ink 2 px above that box, so the window it opens is taller than 4 px (it clears the LARGEST eye box). Over the intro the hood swings down about the crest like a visor, so it never pops on and never sweeps over the eye. The baby has no hood (on a head that big it read as a beret): the sleep bun (tuck 2), its head bowed about 14° over its paws, one `sad` eye open. All stages: `scared` then `sad`, a 1 px tremble every 8 f, the crystals flickering between `glow.sh` and `glow` every 12 f, no "z". The elder tucks at its tempo (a 120 f loop after its 20 f intro) with **no tremble**: a sulk breath twice a loop (the shell rising 0.6 px, eased, no holds), its upset carried by `scared` → `sad` and the crystals' flicker.
- **Adult-only extra:** the 3-crystal cluster.
- **Elder (v2).** The dome grows to 36 × 12 with 6 facets, whole; the head drops about 2 px (half the elder's neck lowering, 2.3), staying 6 px under the dome's top. The sandstone greys least in the cast (scale weight 0.25, D28): it must keep its simulated saturation against the sea-green water under protanopia. Its age shows where the colour is free to move: the **dome** greys at 1.3 as an elder (`#7e5f44` → `#706359`, S 0.46 → 0.21, still chromatic, so the dim crystals keep their hue pass on it, gate h; at full weight, `#746255`, and the shared 0.6 silver on the crown, rock was the least greyed elder in the cast: cast review v2 round 2), the cream belly at 1.5 (`#fff5cc` → `#f8f5e8`), the crown silvered at 0.8 (`#eae4d7`), the slate wings, and the elder face: a white old dog's muzzle and tuft `#efefed` (L 0.86; 0.72, `#dddddb`, was a pale smudge: 2.5) and a **white chin tuft**, the beard in the muzzle white (its mid stone `#afafae` was a separate grey lump under the white chin, a stone or a leaf: cast review v2 round 2, 5.6 E15). **Elder-only extra: the fourth crystal** (the table): permanent, one more than an adult of its bond, a cluster of 4 at full bond ("the fourth crystal" stays the name of the elder's one-more, whichever of the cluster's places it grows in).
- **Elder: the airing is ROCK SUNNING ITS CRYSTALS** (act `airing`, 106 f on the airing's beats; its own override, which its elder pool schedules once in six, reminisce once: 4.2).
  - It **sits up** over 24 f to **18°**, the rump down on the straw in a kick of dust (the rig's floor guard stops the body on the hind thighs from about 13°), the chest up, the front legs straight, the paws planted (the hind paws drawn in 4 px, both front paws back 2 px, so the far front leg keeps its place ahead of the near one: drawn back alone it hid behind the near leg but for a 1 px dark stick). 18° is the most rock's short front legs allow: at 20° the front paws leave the floor on every combination of paw slides.
  - The tail lies along the floor; the neck rises 6°; the face tips up to −20° in the world; the slow breath moves to the squash (the floor guard holds the seated body still).
  - **Eyes open**, gazing up (the elder blink running), while the crystals **soak up the sun**: standing upright (no lean), they fill with `glow.hi` root to tip in two steps (lower half, then whole), rear to front 5 f apart, timed so the front one is whole at the **full charge** (f 51) at any bond. The full charge glints on every tip at once for 6 f, and the face goes `happy` only then; no sweep glint or twinkle runs through it. Full is held until it pushes itself up (f 80), then they drain together tip first (the lower half for 5 f, then none).
  - Its stubby wings never leave the rim.
  - It is the sunbathe fidget's cousin, never its copy: sitting up where the fidget sprawls flat, open-eyed while it charges where the fidget shuts its eyes from the start, charging crystals where the fidget only glints. (The first build sat back 12° with its face `happy` throughout: too close to the sunbathe, the v2 element review.) The grow-up's "the fourth crystal flashes" can borrow the soak and the all-tips glint.
- **Signature: Gravel Roar.**
  - **Tell:** the crystals flash `glow.hi` for 4 f, twice, and the head lowers.
  - **Blast:** jaw at 25° (young 22), the eyes `closed` for the snap's first 8 f.
    - A billowing cone of 5 dust puffs: `scale`, **opaque**, r 3 → 7 as they travel, no ink, each ringed 1 px in `scale.sh` (the adult's; plain sand discs were a pale smudge 27 % from the straw, and overlapping, the ringed lobes read as one billow), then shrinking away in 3 steps. Never alpha: at α 0.85 the dust sat 23 % from the floor. One is born every 5 f (young 6), the last at cue 20 (young 12); each travels 7 + reach × (1 − (1 − age / 30)²) px, reach 26 (young 20), so the cloud billows and holds 10 to 30 px from the mouth. The jaw shuts 3 f after the last birth, the head jitter running until then.
    - 4 pebbles: 4 × 4 and 3 × 3 by turns in `marking`, ink-outlined, the 4 × 4s **faceted** (the bottom row and right column in `marking.sh`, a lit 3 × 3 top-left face; like a crystal's, a facet only from 4 px: 5.2; all flat 3 × 3 they were small brown dots), ballistic (gravity 0.15 px/f²), bouncing once; lying pebbles shrink a size, then another, then are gone over the breath's last 6 f. The young's stay 3 × 3 and flat, its puffs unringed (half strength).
    - The root jitters ±1 px for 12 f.
  - **Young:** 3 puffs and 2 pebbles.
  - **Baby:** "ptoo": one puff of cheek-dust (r 3 → 2, 2 px clear of the snout, 8 f), a single pebble drops out (shrinking away like the roar's), then `happy` with the chin raised 6° (proud).
  - **Elder** (84 f, 4.2's elder column): the roar at the elder's tempo. The tell lowers the head 11° (0.8×) while the crystals flash twice; the eyes shut 10 f at the snap; jaw 25°. The push is the shared elder recoil (1 px, eased, home over the finale), and the effort is ONE slow swell of the head (−2° → −3° → −2°) through the stream: never a root jitter or a head jitter, the tremor D21 rejects (the first build's root "settling back 1 px on 3 f beats" and 1.5° head jitter every 5 f oscillated: the v2 element review). 5 puffs, one every 6 f, at 1.1× the reach (29). **Finale:** the other puffs have shrunk away by cue 38 (f 60); the last puff opens into ONE RING of dust, a 2 px band of opaque sand edged 1 px in `scale.sh` outside and in, outer radius 7 → 10, drifting up 12 px and on 4 px over 24 f; it narrows to 1 px for its last 4 f and is gone at f 82. The elder lifts its head 5° to watch it, then shows `happy` with a 2° nod (72 to 84).
- **Idle: the weight shift.** Young and adult idle for two breaths a loop (adult 240 f, young 200: the shared breath's keys exactly), and in the second the heavy body shifts forward onto the near front paw (it lifts 1.5 px, sets down 2 px ahead, the chest dips 1°) and back a second later. The baby keeps the shared bob (a weight shift under a 4 px leg is a wobble). The elder lays it over the shared elder idle (480 f): the set-down 2 px ahead at f 190 in its second breath, back at f 265, a lift of 1.5 px, a chest dip of 0.8°, a dust kick at each.
- **Ambient** (the per-dragon 6 of 5.4 shared by the twinkle, the dust and the crumb: each dust BIT is one particle, a kick of 2 or 3 pairs is 4 or 6, and only the bits granted are drawn):
  - A crystal glint every 200 ± 60 f (the twinkle, above).
  - A 2 × 2 dust kick (opaque `scale`) at a paw on each weight shift: every other hind contact of the walk (the near hind's), the idle's set-down, and wherever the heavy body lands (the belly settling into sleep or the sunbathe, the shell landing in the roll-over, the paws coming back down).
  - A pebble crumb rolls off the dome every 300 ± 60 f while it stands about (idle, variants, pet, beg).
  - Idle fidget: it **sunbathes** flat (120 f; young × 0.85, baby × 0.6), the opposite of the sleep in every channel that reads at game scale: its belly flops onto the floor with its legs sprawled (hind paws back, front paws forward: a basking lizard, where a sleeper tucks them under), the tail laid straight out behind, the chin raised to the sun with the eyes shut in bliss, two slow deep breaths, the crystals soaking up the sun (mood +1: lit, glinting), no "z", then it pushes itself up. Its face is `happy` (the "^" arcs and the blush ARE eyes closed), chosen over `closed` so it never reads as asleep.

### 3.5 LIGHTNING: "Zap", the storm dragon
**Identity and care.**
- Hyper, zippy and curious. It needs play and exercise.
- **Static builds from boredom:** with no play for a while, its crackles come faster (ambient, below), and the next touch discharges it: it zaps you (`zap`, 4.2), then looks `sheepish`. Play drains the charge. (Spike reacts to *where* you pet it, so the two never punish the same mistake.)
- It naps in short bursts, like a lamp being switched off.

**Why the body is blue and not yellow.** Yellow scales would sit within 20° of fire and rock. A cobalt body with a yellow signal colour reads "electric" instantly, because yellow on blue is the storm pairing (R and G agreed).

**The cue: bolt wings, held cocked.**
- **Pose.** At rest the wings stand cocked at **115°** (in the 1.1 convention: measured from +x forward, positive up, so 115° leans 25° *back* from vertical) with the spars closed. The yellow membrane, with a zigzag trailing edge on a blue body, reads as a lightning bolt.
- **Root.** 3 px behind the generic wing root of 2.1: young (+1, −8), adult (+2, −9). The baby's nubs root at (−3, −6), over the hips, because its head covers everything from x −1.5 forward (the generic root at (+1, −6) is *inside* the baby cranium).
- **Adult polygon.** In wing space (root at (0, 0), +x forward, up negative), as drawn at the rest cock:
  - leading edge: (0, 0) → (−3, −13) → (−8, −28);
  - zigzag trailing edge: (−8, −28) → (−14, −17) → (−10, −17) → (−15, −8) → (−11, −8) → (−8, 0);
  - one 3 px leading spar in `scale` (young 2 px), clipped to its bolt and running up the leading edge from the root to 70 % of the height, so the spire's top 30 % is yellow at every mood (unclipped, its round cap left the top 5 px bare blue). Both bolts are flat, one tone each, inked (no cel band); the far bolt carries no spar.
  - It is a simple polygon, 15 × 28. Each zigzag shelf ends 5.7 and 9.2 px behind the leading edge, so the 3 px spar plus at least 2 px of yellow survive at both steps.
- **Cock.** The polygon rotates about its root by (cock − 115°): sad 140° keeps 79 % of the rest height, excited 95° reaches 104 %.
- **Young polygon.** Hand-authored at 16 × 10: leading edge (0, 0) → (−2, −7) → (−5, −16); trailing edge (−5, −16) → (−10, −9) → (−7.5, −9) → (−10, −4) → (−7.5, −4) → (−5, 0). Its shelves end 4.8 and 6.4 px behind the leading edge. The adult's scaled to 16 × 9 ended them 3.4 and 5.5 px back, so only 0 to 2 px of yellow survived past the 2 px spar, the far bolt's olive outweighed the yellow, and the sprout read as a brown pine cone (at 16 × 7, before that, the yellow was weak too).
- **Far wing.** Offset (−4, −2) (young (−2.5, −2); the baby's nub (−1, −1), inside the near one) and turned **+8°** (further back), so a double bolt shows. As the cock rises from rest toward 95° it also drops up to 3 px, so the near tip stays the spire's peak when excited (at 95° the far tip stood 1.5 px higher). The **elder's** far bolt also steps up to 3 px further back with that drop (`farBack`): dropped alone, its three teeth fell between the near bolt's; without the drop, its tip stood 2 px above the near one's.
- **Clearance** (rest solve, horns rooted at the top-back of the cranium): the tip stays ≥ 10 px from the horn tips at rest and when sad (adult 21 px, young 11). Excited, it comes to 14 px (adult) and 6 px (young), which is when sparks jump between them. The polygon never comes within 6 px of the cranium in any mood.
- **Folding.** The wings **never fold flat**. Asleep they drop to the sad cock (140°) with the spars closed, and the horns keep their colour, so the sleeping silhouette still names the element (5.1 #1).

| Feature | Baby | Young | Adult | Elder (weathered) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bolt wings, h × w, notches, rest cock | 2 bolt nubs, 12 × 5, one zigzag step 2 px deep at 55 % of the height, a pointed tip, at 105° from (−3, −6): the only upright shape behind a baby's head (about 37 px² of it clears head and body). The far nub tucks INSIDE the near one's outline, (−1, −1) net (`farDx` −1, `farDy` 1), so the seed is one spire with an olive rim: stepped 4 px back it stood apart as a second 12 px tooth, and the pair gave the baby a two- or three-point top edge at /3, the saw language on spike's back line | 16 × 10, 2 notches, 115° | 28 × 15, 2 notches, 115° | **28 × 16.5**: the adult's height (the elder's 1.02 height cap), 1.1× as wide, with a **third tooth** (the elder-only extra); rest cock 115°, the adult's; a tear on the far bolt's trailing edge, shown when the storm-watch opens the pair (2.9), no chipped tooth |
| Horns: 2, swept back along the neck line, with one kink at 60 % of their length | 3 px buds, pointing up and back | 5 px, r 1.5 → 1 | 8 px, r 1.5 → 1 | as the adult |
| Back row | none | none | none | none |
| Tail | thin, straight and stiff, 0.65 as long, lifted a perky 12°; a plain taper to a true point (tip r 0.5); motion is stepped (4 f holds) | the same, full length, lifted 5° (level it was a ruler-straight rod, and its round-capped tip read as a spear's end) | same | as the adult, lifted 5°, still stepped |
| Markings: yellow Z-stripes, 3 px thick, below the wing root, ≥ 3 px above the belly line (preferred; 2.7) | 1 on the **tail base**, 5 × 5 (the baby's shoulder is under its head; a 6 px Z never fits whole in its thin tail) | tail base 6 × 6 + haunch 6 × 7 (a 5-wide Z has no step at any height and reads as a slash) | tail base 7 px + haunch and shoulder, 6 × 8 (the lean flank between the leg roots and the back holds no 9 px Z whole) | as the adult, greyed at half strength |
| Build | lean (depth 0.85), long legs (1.2), head high | same | same | same, the head about 4 px lower |

- **Mood:** cock 140° (sad; the spire keeps 79 % of its rest height) → 115° → 95° (excited). The baby nubs run 125° → 105° → 95°. The elder's (v2) run the adult's 140° → 115° → 95° (EL's 118° rest leaned toward the sad 140°).
- **Elder-only extra (v2): the third tooth**, permanent at every mood: the elder bolt keeps the adult's leading edge (0, 0) → (−3, −13) → (−8, −28) and height, and its trailing edge steps three times, (−8, −28) → (−15, −20) → (−11, −20) → (−16, −12) → (−12, −12) → (−16.5, −5) → (−12.5, −5) → (−8.5, 0): each shelf ends 5.7, 9.2 and 11.3 px behind the leading edge (the adult's: 5.7 and 9.2), so the 3 px spar and ≥ 2 px of yellow survive at every step, and the storm has grown a tooth. Its teeth sit 7 to 8 px apart, about 2.5 px at ÷ 3, and keep the spire's zigzag (checked in the element pass: no comb). **Ambient (elder):** at `mood` ≥ 0.5, every 150 ± 40 f (1.5× slower in a crowd), the spark glyph hops from the near bolt's tip to a point 5 px over the gap and down onto the far tip, 3 f a hop (9 f; 5.1 #12's keys); it shares the crackle's budget and stands in for the tip crackle while it lives. As a stroke run tip to tip over the 5 to 7 px between the tips it lay on them as a lid, and bowed over them it was a hoop (a yellow bag handle). Its body greys at half strength (so it stays clear of dusk's dark blue, 3.9), its membrane too (it is the signal); a tear on the far bolt's trailing edge shows when the storm-watch opens the pair (2.9): a notch 6.5 px wide at the edge, its lower side stepped 1.5 px halfway in, its sides meeting in a **point**, a rip (cast review v2: with a flat bottom it was a clean square chunk out of the bolt's back edge, read as a rendering bite, not wear).
- **Elder: the airing is a STORM-WATCH** (106 f, act `airing`, its own override; 4.2): chest up 8°, body up 2 px, the snout 18° up (mostly head pitch: raised on the neck, the horn met the near bolt), eyes OPEN and neutral, the tail held out; `happy` only as the pair closes (cue 72 to 94). The bolts rise to 95°, the near one tipping 5° forward and the far one fanning 32° back, so the V opens from the tips: the far bolt's straight leading edge shows about 10 px above the overlap, the facing edges 9 to 13 px apart at the rungs (at a tip of 7° the horn came within 1 px of the near bolt). A slow Jacob's ladder: rung 1 at cue 44 from the near bolt's first tooth point, rung 2 at cue 54, 4 px higher, each a 3-segment zigzag stopped 2 px short of both bolts, living 6 f and flipping after 3; at cue 64 it breaks off the top, a spark off each tip, 4 then 7 px up. The far bolt's tear opens with the V (2.9). Fans of 20° (the first build), 45° and 60° failed: at 20° the far bolt lay under the near one's teeth as an olive fir tree, 3 to 5 px between them; at 45° and 60° it drooped over the rump. A rung across the V closes off its bottom below it as a small pale pocket for its 6 f.
- **Adult-only extra:** **charge**. Above `mood` 0.5 one spark crawls from the near horn's tip to a bolt tip, the near and the far one in turn, in 6 hops of 3 f (18 f), every 90 ± 30 f (1.5× slower in a crowd), sharing the ambient budget with the crackle. Crawling every frame, a well-kept adult (above 0.5 most of its life) flickered all the time and never rested.
- **Signature: Spark Bolt.**
  - **Wind-up:** the wings flare to 95°. Spark crawlers (the spark glyph, below) hop between the horn tips and wing tips every 3 f for 18 f, and the **pupil contracts** (the stepped pose channel `pupil`, keyed by `tuning.breath.pupil` until the snap: 2.5).
  - **Bolt:** a 4-segment polyline from the mouth, with segment lengths 10 / 8 / 10 / 8 (about 28 px out; at 8 / 6 / 8 / 6 it was the young's 20 px zigzag again, and the adult breath no reward beside fire's flame), each kinked 40 to 49° the other way from the last (seeded and re-rolled every 4 f): at ±30° it read as a bent straw, at 50 to 60° it folded into a 16 px "N". It is drawn as a filled zig polygon in the signal yellow `membrane` `#ffcf33` with a 1 px ink outline, 4.5 px at the mouth tapering to 60 % (2.7 px) at the tip (tapered to a point, its end was an ink hair; to 40 % of 3.5 px, a hair-thin last segment), plus an 8 px, 3 px wide fork off the second kink, a 1.6 px hot core of `glow` down all but its last segment (no ink), and a 5 × 5 impact diamond at the tip. Filled in the pale `glow` `#fff6a0` it was carried by its ink alone, a pale stick. Lifetime 20 f.
  - **Flash:** on the snap's first cue step (2 f; young and adult) the whole body is drawn flat in `glow.hi` **inside its own ink**, the bolt and every effect keeping their colours over it: the grow-up's flat flash (4.2) with the outline kept, so the shape reads on the pale straw. The element asks for it through `ElementSpec.tint` (a tint of 1 is the opaque flash; the rig draws it straight, `rig.keepInk`). Any partial `glow` over the blue body came out grey: at α 0.35 held 2 f a ghosted dragon, at α 0.5 a grey-blue dropout (the cast review, round 2); flat without its ink it was a pale ghost on the floor.
  - **After the bolt** (cue 20–29): the impact becomes one spark for 3 f, which splits into 3 (young 2) at 6 px, then 10 px, on 3 f steps, fanned 75° apart; meanwhile a bare 2 × 2 of static flickers between the open jaws (mouth-space x −1..1) every 3 f. The adult breath is 62 f: its sustain is cut to 22 f, so the jaw starts closing at cue 28 (anim f 46) and the recover stays 16 f.
  - **Young:** 3 segments of 6 / 5 / 6 kinked 45–51°, no fork and no core, and the 5 × 5 impact diamond. At 2 segments it read as a pipe or a bone in the mouth, and at 5 / 4 / 5 with a 3 × 3 pip in pale `glow`, still as a bone.
  - **Baby:** a 4 f "static pop". One 2-segment spark from the nose, every feature raised +1 px (hair on end), the nubs 2 px more, the tail snapped straight up (lift −80, stiff) for the pop's 4 f, then `dazed`. Its dark baby eye does not contract.
- **The spark glyph.** Every spark but the jaw static is a 4 × 6 **stepped bolt** of `membrane` yellow (in the pale `glow` it was nearly the dazed stars' white, and the baby's pop sparks and its dazed stars ran together), rows 0011 / 0110 / 1110 / 0111 / 0110 / 1100, in a 1 px ink outline on 4-neighbours only (an 8-neighbour ring filled the notches into a blob), mirrored on each hop or beat. It replaces the flat 2 × 2 (and the `scale` ring of the sparks that reach the floor: the ink outline carries them), and the "+" of an earlier round, whose 1 px arms read as the dazed star (5.2).
- **Ambient:**
  - Crackle: a spark on a bolt tip for 4 f (on the horn's tip it sat where the dazed stars circle, and read as one), which hops 3 px for 4 more, every 120 ± 40 f, faster as the boredom charge builds (`DragonInfo.charge` 0 → 1, fed by the owner: down to every 40 ± 13 f), every 30 ± 10 f when excited. The crackle and the charge pause while it is happy, begging or breathing.
  - Idle fidget: **zoomies**, dashing 40 px and back (50 f; young 32 px, 43 f; baby 18 px, 30 f), the bolts swept back to 130° (baby 125°) over the gallop's first 6 f and snapped to 95° at the skid's "whoa"; it springs back in 3 happy bounces rather than turning (a run backwards read as a moonwalk). The elder's zoomies are a **lope**, the adult's gallop at × 1.3 / 0.8× (a 13 f cycle, paw lift 4.8, rise 1.6), 28 px in 60 f: no "whoa" (happy from the skid), and the 3 bounces are a chuckle (jaw 16° a hop), its gestures at 0.8×.

### 3.6 WATER: "Ripple", the tide dragon
**Identity and care.**
- Gentle, curious and playful.
- It loves baths: a bath is instant happiness.
- Its spots dim when it dries out or is sad.
- It sings in bubbles, follows toys and floats belly-up in the pond.

**Colour (v2): sea green** (D27). The body is "light sea green" `#28b0a6` (H 176, S 0.77, L 0.34), its fins and fluke a deep sea green `#185e5b` (0.26× the body's luminance, inside D11's range), its nostril `#0c2a2a`, its blush `#ffbccb`; the sea-foam belly, pearl horn and spots, aqua glow and gold iris are kept. SW's search over the gates, with the dusk dragon in the cast and no pair allowed to separate on hue alone: **no water with hue ≤ 170 and S > 0.42 passes**. A vivid true sea green (`#2aa88a`) turns into a near-grey at fire's value under deuteranopia (2 % apart); darker, the fire pair separates on hue alone; lighter, it fails rock under protanopia. Hue 173 to 178 is the greenest a vivid water can be.

**The user's choice (v2 review).** `#28b0a6` is 0.007 Oklab ΔE from CSS "lightseagreen", a colour many players call teal or turquoise, so before it is final the user sees three options on the rig, `shots/v2/water_sea_green_options.png` (top to bottom, each baby / young / adult):
1. **light sea green `#28b0a6`** (the default): the greenest vivid water that passes every gate; reads teal to some eyes.
2. **true sea green `#2aa88a`** (H 166): reads plainly sea green; fails the deuteranopia gate against fire (the two bodies 2 % apart for about 1 player in 16), so choosing it means moving fire or water's value and re-running the whole cast.
3. **jade `#68a794`** (H 162, S 0.38): greener and passes, but the least saturated body in the cast, dusty beside the others, and it greys toward the chromatic floor as an elder.

The membrane stays the deep sea green `#185e5b` (the fluke is the cue, so it carries the "sea" in every option): the readability review proposed a bluer `#16566e` to part it from spike's leaf membrane (0.045 ΔE), but beside the revised dusk body `#1f5580` that lands 0.019 ΔE from the dusk dragon's own body; the care review proposed a greener one, which lands on spike's leaf. Instead water's fins grey at 0.6 and spike's leaf at 0.6, so their elders do not converge on one grey. Measured against the v1 cyan: scale.sh / membrane 29 → 43 %, lightning / water now passes on hue and value (it was value only). Nothing in water's construction changes: the fins and fluke take the new membrane, pearl rays sit 90 % from it, bubble and drip rings 87 % from the floor.

**The cue: the fluke tail.** A vertical crescent fluke at the end of the longest, flattest, level body in the cast (the body and tail form a bar about 3.5 : 1).
- **Construction.** The fluke is `membrane` with 2 tones (no highlight) and 2 px rays in `horn` that radiate from the tail's end, just outside its contour, toward each lobe tip (from 2.5 px out they were 2 × 3 stubs by the lobe's back edge). Rays, the fin-ear ray and the lobe dots are whole-pixel runs in face space (`pixelStroke`): a 2 px stroke in the rotated tail-tip space anti-aliased into mush. The rays sit on 10° angle buckets, so they no longer crawl as the tail sways. The adult's glow dot sits at (−depth + 3.5, ± (h/2 − 2.5)) at the REST height, so it stays whole when the lobes flare, and its ray stops so ≥ 1 px of membrane shows between them; the dots are stamped after the fluke's clip is restored, so each is always a whole 2 × 2 (inside the clip the lower one lost a corner at rest: the element pass v2). There is no ink line across the tail / fluke joint.
- **Notch.** A central notch ≥ 3 px deep keeps both lobes readable.

| Feature | Baby | Young | Adult | Elder (weathered) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fluke, h × depth | paddle 10 × 6, no rays, on a thinner tail (r 3.5 → 1.5, 2.3): a tadpole paddle clearly wider than the tail that carries it, ONE rounded leaf in one flat `membrane` tone, widest two-thirds of the way out, its back end round. Its top and bottom edges leave the tail's own contour 6 px before the tip and swell out along it, drawn only outside the tail and its ink, so the tail runs into the fin. A disc hung on the tip read as a lollipop; notched at the back, banded and with the tail run on through it, it split into two round dark lobes, a dog-bone or a club end | crescent 11 × 6, 2 rays | crescent 16 × 8, 2 rays, notch ≥ 3 px, and a 2 × 2 `glow` dot set 2 px in from each lobe tip (the tips themselves are under 3 px wide). A third ray down the middle fills the notch, and a lobe 4 to 5 px thick holds one 2 px ray: the adult's extra is the dots | **18 × 9**, grown, whole, notch 4 (≥ 3); its lobes fuller than the adult's, most of all in their outer half (trailing-edge control (1.0 D, 0.2 h/2): straight back from the tip, turning into the notch only near the middle; leading-edge control (0.15 D, 1.0 h/2)): a broad old whale's fluke; **2 rays**, starting at the tail's tip; the lobe dots grown into **3 × 3 pearls** in `horn` (the elder-only extra), centred 3.5 px in from the lobe tip along the fluke and 4 across at the rest height (2 px in at their nearest edge, where the adult's dot sits), each at the end of its lobe's ray with ≥ 1 px of membrane between them and all round the pearl; the pearl's 4 corners in horn's shadow tone (`#9aaac3`), so the 3 × 3 reads as a round bead (with a bottom-right L it read as a die). (The element pass's first build dropped the rays for the pearls, and the elder's cue looked plainer than the adult's: the v2 element review.) |
| Fin-ear: ONE, drawn behind the neck and skull (at `farHead`, in the near palette: only its contour-breaking part shows), riding on the neck, its lower edge along the neck's top contour and its root hidden behind the back of the skull; a small fan with a webbed trailing edge (two shallow scallops and a middle point). The far ear is not drawn (1.5: it was a sliver cap over the near one), and a round lobe read as an ear-cup or a headphone. Length past the skull × height: | 4.5 × 4 | 5 × 4, no ray | 7 × 6, 1 ray (from the skull's contour to the edge's middle point) that shows ONLY when it flares: at rest a 2 × 4 white bar in a small dark fan read as a headphone highlight or a cat's inner ear | as the adult |
| Horns | none: a smooth, domed forehead | none | none | none |
| Dorsal fin: low, continuous, round scallops, `membrane` | none | 2 px, 2 scallops | 3 px, 3 scallops (inside spike's zone budget) | as the adult |
| Wings: fin-wings with a rounded, smooth trailing edge and no scallops | nub | 3 rays | 4 rays | 4 rays; ragged 5 × 4 notches between rays 1–2 (the fin's rounded back end at the resting spread) and 3–4, and a notched 4 × 3 hole against the forearm at full spread (2.9) |
| Markings: pearl spots, 3 × 3, on the lateral line, ≥ 3 px apart. The first sits low on the rear flank at every stage (2.7); the rest run forward along the flank and back along the tail's side, and the pet seed steps each one after the first 1 px across the line, **alternating** up and down, so the row never reads as a dashed stripe or as pairs at one height. The flank anchor is the lateral line itself, halfway down from the back to the belly line. The last tail spot is authored `dx` −2 (it predates the tail fit's counting of the seeded `dx`: 2.7) | 2 (flank + tail) | 3: 1 flank + 2 tail (t 0.18, 0.52): the young's visible flank between the folded wing and the leg roots is one spot tall, and two flank spots fitted side by side at one height on every seed, a pair | 5 (3 flank + 2 tail at t 0.2 / 0.45) | 5, as the adult |
| Build | long (1.1), low legs (0.8), long neck (1.3), long level tail (1.15) | same | same | same, the neck × 0.92 (2.3) |

- **Mood.** Three spot states, each ≥ 40 % from the next (gate h):
  - `mood` ≥ +0.5: spots swap to `glow` `#40d8f0` (40 % from the resting spot; the first draft's `#7ff4ff` was only 18 % from it, and the marking is too grey for hue to count), and the fin-ear **flares**: +10° over the neck line and 2 px longer, with its ray. (Open, for the bible owner: `#40d8f0`, luminance about 0.57, is darker than the resting pearl `#dcfff6`, about 0.95, so happy spots read duller, not lit. Proposed: at +1 keep the pearl and add a flat 1 px `glow` halo, a 4-neighbour ring, round each spot, which needs the rig to draw a ring round fitted markings or `markingTone` to return a fill and a ring.)
  - rest: spots in `marking` `#dcfff6`.
  - `mood` ≤ −0.3 (dry or sad) and asleep (**asleep at any mood**, pulsing one state up, to `marking`, at the top of each breath: 4.3's pulse; before, a pet asleep at rest mood kept pearl spots that pulsed to the happy glow): spots dim to `moodTones().dimSpot` `#82d8ce` (38 % from the resting spot, 41 % from the scale; v1's cyan gave `#7ed1e2`, 41 / 44 %), the fin-ear droops (8° below the neck line, folded to ¾ length, no ray; standing, the 40° world clamp takes it further) and the fluke droops 15°. The fluke keeps its full size. At rest the ear sits 4° over the neck line; where the neck rises steeply behind the head (eat, asleep) it lifts 4° at a time until it clears the neck, so asleep it lies up-back along the nape, about 20° above straight back.
- **Adult-only extra:** the lobe glow dots and the dorsal fin.
- **Elder (v2).** The fluke grows to 18 × 9, whole (the table); the neck is capped (× 0.92) so the elder is ≤ 1.08× the adult's length. Water greys at 0.8 with its luminance kept (3.9): darkened as it greys (SW's −0.3 drift), its elder met fire's value under deuteranopia; its fins at 0.6; its elder face is a sea-foam frost (2.5). **Elder-only extra: the pearls**, permanent at every mood: the adult's 2 × 2 lobe dots grown to 3 × 3 pearls in `horn` `#eaf6f0` on the fluke's membrane (90 %; the pearl-white horn is also its rays and spots' family), "the old tide dragon's pearls". (The first draft's barnacles, inked 3 × 2 cones on the tail top, left a 1 px top row under the mark floor, read as ticks or warts, and used spike's pale-points language in spike's zone: the v2 review.)
- **Signature: Bubble Jet.**
  - **Wind-up:** cheeks puff (head squash-x 1.1) and the fin-ear flares.
  - **Stream:** a bubble every 4 f (young 6) from cue 3 over the whole sustain: the adult's 9, r 2 to 5 with one r 6 at the jet's middle (radii 3, 2, 4, 2, 6, 2, 5, 3, 4; its 8 of r 2 to 5 were a few small bubbles beside fire's flame, the cast review, round 2), the young's 4, r 2 to 3; along the snout at 2.5 px/f, life 18 f + a 2 f pop, in a seeded cone of ± 8 to 12° on alternating sides, so the rings keep ≥ 2 px of air between them. Each swells from r 1 by 1 px every 2 f as it leaves the lip.
    - Each rises with a ±1 px wobble, stepped every 4 f.
    - A bubble is a `glow` fill at α 0.45, a 1 px ring in `membrane` (so it reads on a pale floor) and a 2 × 2 `#f8f4ec` highlight at the top-left.
    - A pop is 2 f of a 4-dot star, skipped within 2 px of a live bubble's ring.
  - **Droplets:** each 2 × 3 in `glow` with a 1 px `membrane` ring, shed off the lower jaw 30 to 60° below level, UNDER the stream (adult 4 at cues 5, 13, 21, 29; young 2), landing as ringed dots for 6 f. Thrown up and forward, they crossed the bubbles as beads.
  - **Young:** the same stream every 6 f.
  - **Baby** (66 f): one big bubble grows on its own snout, r 2 → 6, wobbles, pops in its face (a pop star), a 4-drop splash (straight up, a forward arc, a quick forward-down, a short hop back by the chin: each with its own flight time, 20 / 13 / 8 / 11 f, and gravity solved for it, all down by cue 54, lying 4 f; none thrown up and back into the eye box), a scrunch and a sneeze-back with the eyes shut, then `happy` with the tongue out. The face stays `neutral` while the bubble grows (the shared `surprised` eye is a flat square on a baby).
- **Ambient:**
  - A drip (2 × 3 `glow` with a 1 px `membrane` ring; bare `glow` sat 12 % from the floor) falls 8 px from the chin and splashes into 2 ringed dots, every 180 ± 60 f, awake, standing still and at `mood` > −0.3. The fin-ear tip is used only where it hangs clear of the body in x (in practice never when standing); a source over the body skips its drip.
  - The spots "breathe", swapping `marking` → `glow` for 30 f every 240 f, stepped, **only at `mood` ≥ 0**, so a sad water dragon never flashes happy.
  - Idle fidget: a dog-style shake that throws droplets: 36 f × the stage's duration + a 10 f settle that is never scaled (baby 32, young 41, adult 46, elder 57, every gesture of the elder's (the brace, the whips, the wobble, the rock, the neck dip) at `FIDGET_TIMING`'s 0.8×), with a head whip, a body wobble and a tail whip, then a happy settle; droplets adult 8, young 6, baby 4, each with gravity solved to land by L − 4.

### 3.7 SLINKWING: "Echo", the night-singer

> **Renamed.** This element was designed as **shriekscale** and renamed **slinkwing** at the user's request; the design below is unchanged by the rename. Where it says "shriek" (the breath, the `shriek` event) or "shriek-scales" (the adult ear ribs), that is the dragon's voice, not its element name.
>
> **This element is an interpretation.** The user gave only the name ("shriekscale", at the time). The reading here:
>
> - "Shriek" means the voice is the element, so slinkwing is a **sound dragon**. It is a nocturnal, echolocating cave dragon, a mix of bat, fennec fox and frilled lizard.
> - Its big **ribbed ear-fans** catch sound, and flare into a dish when it shrieks.
> - Its sound is drawn as **expanding arcs**.
> - "Scale" is also read as a **musical scale**. When happy it sings a rising 3-note scale, and its adult ear ribs are the "shriek-scales" that rattle when it screams.
>
> **Why this reading:**
> 1. Sound is the only one of the six elements that is naturally invisible. It needs a body part that makes it visible (the fans) and an effect (the arcs).
> 2. The **head** is the one silhouette region no other element uses. Head-doubling fans make it as instantly readable at 24 px as the other five.
> 3. Big ears are the strongest baby-animal cue there is (fennec kits, bat pups), so the baby slinkwing doubles as the mascot.
> 4. Sound gives it a voice in the care loop: it chirps when content, shrieks when lonely and sings when happy.
>
> If the user meant something else, two things change: the palette column and the head-zone cue. Everything else is parameters.

**Identity and care.**
- Dramatic, clingy and nocturnal: **the night owl** of the cast (dusk is the early sleeper, 3.8).
- It shrieks for attention when lonely, chirps when content and sings when happy.
- (v2, the rename.) "Slink" is given a motion of its own: its walk is a low, crouched, stop-and-go bat crawl (4.3), so the name fits the dragon as well as the wings.
- Loud noises make it flatten its fans.
- It is the hardest to settle, and it bonds the deepest.

**The cue: ear-fans.**
- **Construction.** Membrane on 2 px `horn` ribs, the outline **polar about a pivot** 1.5 / 3 / 4 px under the root (baby / young / adult), so the ribs spread from below the root. The young and adult roots sit on the skull's top-back at 128° and 0.88 r, behind the eye, so the fans never cover it and rise clear of the skull (rooted at about 0.62 r, a third of the fan lay over the skull and the pair read as mouse ears). The baby's ear is a **fennec leaf** 10 × 8 on the crown (100°, 0.84 r): a 6 px root, widest a little above it and narrowing to a tip about 5 px across (its outline falls away within ±15° of the leaf's middle: spread over ±50°, it was a round disc, a mouse ear), 8° more upright than the others, so it reads as an ear on the head and stays well clear of baby lightning's bolt nubs over the hips (a 10 × 10 disc barely changed between moods and read as a mouse).
- **Ribs** radiate from the root and spread over the **whole free edge**, from the tall front-top to the short back. As enforced (5.2): a rib is drawn only where each of its 2 × 2 whole-pixel stamps (`pixelStroke`) has **2 full pixels of membrane on all four sides**, checked at every mood, in the dish and the rattle, asleep and through the idle breath; the ribs start about 3.6 px apart (centre to centre). The baby's one rib is laid from the root up the leaf's middle; the clearance keeps the stamps where the leaf is wide enough, about 4 px of its upper half. The far fan has no ribs (1.5). **Young and adult: the ribs are PLEATS**, tone planes rather than stamps: sectors from the pivot out, clipped to the fan, laid in `membrane.sh` between rib lines (adult: front rib to middle rib, and back rib to the back edge; young: front rib to the scallop's front cusp), the rest flat `membrane`, no cel band (the pleats are the fan's form). Each pleat edge runs out to a scallop's cusp like a bat wing's spar to its point, and the pleats bend with the dish's spread and the sleeping curl. Stamped, the ribs had to start 3.6 px apart and keep 2 px from the narrow base, so the adult's three stood mid-fan as parallel pale slashes (the cast reviews); planes are not bound by 5.2's rib clearance, as rock's dome facets are not. The baby's leaf keeps its one stamped rib.
- **Scallops** are on the NEAR fan only, 3 px deep, running cusp to cusp between the rib ends and behind the last (at 2 px they were a 1 px nick; on the far fan, which has no ribs, they read as a cauliflower edge).
- **The mood gauge** (`flare`):
  - −1 lies 88° back from the head's up; 0 is 40° back (the baby 32°); +1 is upright (the baby 8° forward of it). The `sad` and `scared` faces count as −1 ("loud noises make it flatten its fans").
  - An anim's flare of ± p pulls the gauge that fraction of the way to ± 1 from any mood; the **dish** comes only from flare > 1. Through the shriek's snap the fans open from the fold, not from the mood, so the fold, the snap and the dish look alike at every mood (the resting mood added to the anim's flare left a mood −1 pet with no dish and a mood +1 pet never folding, stuck in the dish).
  - The twitch moves the fans 0.3 toward upright, or back from upright when mood > 0.7.
  - Measured as the silhouette area the fans add (fans on against off, seeds 1, 2 and 5), mood −1 keeps ≥ 76 / 72 / 71 / 66 % (baby / young / adult / elder; the baby's leaf ear of the cast review 78 %) and asleep ≥ 86 / 91 / 85 / 83 %: all over the 60 % floor. **The elder's −1 lies 80° back, not 88°**: its lower, level head let the neck line swallow fans laid 88° back (58 % of the resting silhouette, under D7).
- **The dish.** Young and adult spread their ribs 1.35× (1.3× the area) and tip the rim 8° forward. The baby spreads 1.12 and grows 1.1 (at 1.35 its ear became a mushroom cap). The adult's rib rattle: its pleats step ± 3° every 2 f, neighbours in counter-phase, while fx > 0.5 after the snap.

| Feature | Baby | Young | Adult | Elder (weathered) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ear-fans, h × w, ribs, edge | 10 × 8 fennec leaf, 1 rib, on top of the cranium | 13 px tall, 2 ribs as 1 pleat, 1 scallop 3 px deep on the near fan | 17 px tall, 3 ribs as 2 pleats, 2 scallops 3 px deep on the near fan | **19 px** tall, **4 ribs** (−4° / 14° / 32° / 50°) **as 3 pleats** (front edge → −4°, 14° → 32°, 50° → back edge, in `membrane.sh`), 2 scallops (32° to 50°, 50° to 68°), whole (no nick: wear is the wings' alone); the frosted rim (below) |
| Horns | none: the fans replace them | none | none | none |
| Eye mask in `marking` (lilac `#9a6aa8`), from the snout base to behind the eye (the mint iris sits inside it): a pale "spectacles" patch on the dark head, drawn like the eye as a **pixel bitmap in face space** (3 px behind the eye to 3 px in front, corners cut 1 px), clipped to the skull. A rect in the pitched cranium space stair-stepped into a band that read as a highlight. The ink face marks read on it (97 %) as they do on every other dragon, and it clears the head's shadow (86 %) and highlight (53 %) tones | 6 px tall, present from hatching | 6 px | 7 px | 7 px, met in front by the grey muzzle (53 % apart) |
| Throat sac: a `membrane` bulge 0.6 of the way up the neck from its root, along the throat's normal, drawn by the element at its breath anchor and clipped behind the head; inked only outside the neck's contour (over the neck's skin it is a colour change); shown while shrieking and through the lonely call | swells 3 px | 5 px | 7 px | 7 px |
| Legs | thin, bat-like (leg r × 0.75): flat at every stage, so the far leg never has to clear a shadow band (D8) | same | same | same |
| Jaw | depth 1.3, opens to 40° (the element's jawMax 40 replaces the young's 34) | same | same | depth 1.3, opens to 36° |
| Tail | a whip (r × 0.7), plain taper, no barb | same | same | same: the adult's length (6 × 7, for the 1.10 length cap) and rest |
| Wings | nub | bat, 3 spars, 3 px scallops | bat, 4 spars, 3 px scallops (at 5 px its four dark spars stood well past the membrane, and the spread wing read as a raised hand, a rake), 3 px thumb claw | bat, 4 spars, 3 px scallops, 3 px thumb claw; one ragged tear in the arm panel at 0.55 (the finger panels have no room: 2.9) and a notched 4 × 3 hole against the forearm by the wrist at full spread; its elder preens at 0.9 leaning back 60° (1.3) |
| Markings | eye mask | eye mask + one 5 × 4 "volume-bar" chevron on the tail at t 0.4 | eye mask + a 6 × 5 chevron at t 0.3, then a 5 × 4 at t 0.5, stepping down along the whip ("3 px" is the chevron's band thickness, 5.2) | as the adult |

(The fan heights are 10 / 13 / 17 px, not the first table's 10 / 12 / 16: at those sizes three ribs with 2 px of membrane on every side were 1–2 px specks, and they vanished on a pet whose seeded length variant is −1.)

- **Adult-only extra:** the ribs rattle during a shriek (the pleats ± 3°, every 2 f), plus a 4 × 4 nose-leaf bump in the skull path.
- **Elder (v2).** Fans 19 px with 3 pleats (the table), grown and whole; other head features may then reach 9.5 px, half the elder fan. Its membrane greys at half strength (it is the signal). **Elder-only extra: frosted fan tips**, permanent: a 2 px pigment band in `fanFrostOf` (`#c5c3bf`, halfway from its mid-grey muzzle to the catchlight white) along the near fan's free edge, from its front up to 32° back from the fan's up (where the scallops start), drawn as whole face pixels clipped to the fan (the muzzle's construction), at every mood, asleep and in the dish: 35 % from the membrane, 71 % from `membrane.sh` (the band crosses the pleats: gate a).
- **Signature: Shriek.**
  - **Wind-up:** the fans fold flat over 10 f, the head pulls back, and the throat sac swells (3 / 5 / 7 px).
  - **Release:** the fans snap from the fold to the dish in 4 f with an overshoot, and the jaw opens to 40°. `event: 'shriek'` on the snap frame (cue 0), young and adult only.
  - **Arcs:** 3 sound arcs expand from the mouth, radius 6 → 34 px over 24 f, one spawned every 8 f.
    - Each arc is `membrane` `#ff6fae`, 2 px thick, flat, with a 1 px `scale` outer edge (47 % and 92 % from the floor), whole-pixel stamps in face space. In `glow` at the old alpha steps it sat 23 %, 16 % and 9 % from the floor.
    - An arc fades by **narrowing**, never by alpha: its span steps ± 35° → ± 28° → ± 22° around the facing direction (at ± 15° an arc of r 30 is a straight bar).
  - **Neighbours:** `event: 'shriek'` lets up to 2 nearby dragons flinch (spike bristles). The game does not consume it yet.
  - **Young:** 2 arcs, out to 24 px, ± 35 → ± 28 → ± 22.
  - **Baby:** a squeak: the ears fold flat, the sac swells 3 px, one arc (r 4 → 14, ± 45 → ± 38 → ± 30) on a small dish (spread 1.12, grow 1.1), no `shriek` event; then its fans flop over its eyes for 24 f, a gag (ledger E8), and pop back up with a blink (`sheepish` after it). Flopped, they pivot from the **front** of the crown and tip about 150° forward (−155° landing, −145° after the bounce: the leaf ear, narrower at its tip, needs 10° more than the old disc), so the near fan's lobe hangs down over the eye with only a sliver peeking out behind: tipped −62° from the crown's top they lay on it like a beret and the eye stayed in view.
  - **The lonely call is a different animation** from this trick: the element anim `call` (`ACT.call`, cue from the note's start, the wind-up negative), adult 100 f (20 wind-up, 50 note, 30 settle), young × 0.85, baby × 0.6. The head lifts 18°, the jaw opens to 25, the face is `sad`, and the fans sit at −1 whatever the mood (flare −1); one arc, r 5 → 40 / 30 / 20 px, narrowing ± 35 → ± 22; the sac fills over the wind-up's last 12 f and stays full through the note; no overshoot and no flinch: `event: 'call'` at the note's start, and neighbours turn their heads toward it instead (not consumed yet). The trick keeps the dish, the 3 arcs and the flinch.
- **Arcs, small.** A small arc needs span to show any curve (a 3 × 6 "J", a digit "1", 2 px ticks were what the first chirp, squeak and snore drew). Nose sounds aim along the head, but no more than 12° above or 10° below level. The chirp is r 5 → 10 over 12 f, ± 45 → ± 38 → ± 30; the snore r 6 → 9 over 24 f, ± 50 → ± 42 → ± 34, tipped 20° up.
- **Ambient:**
  - Echolocation chirp from the nose every 150 ± 50 f (through the ambient budget); at `mood` ≥ 0.6 it is replaced by 2 hummed notes every 200 ± 60 f ("chirps when content, sings when happy").
  - Fan twitch: one fan flicks for 6 f, alternating near and far, every 120 ± 40 f.
  - Happy: it **sings** a rising 3-note scale: 3 eighth notes at cue 0 / 8 / 16, each 5 × 7 (a 3 × 3 ball, a 2 px stem and a 1 px flag hooking down) in `glow` with a 4-neighbour ink ring (a solid 2 × 2 flag or an 8-neighbour ring read as a boot or a bean), each starting 10 px further out and 5 px higher than the last and rising 8 px over 40 f in the top pass, in alpha steps.
  - **Idle fidget: the echo-ping** (adult 64 f; young × 0.85, baby × 0.6). The head lifts 15° and leans in 2 px, the fans go upright; at f 12 a "))" pair goes out, 5 px apart; at f 30 a "((" pair comes back, centred 30 / 26 / 20 px ahead; at f 43 the fans flick to flare 1.25; at f 45, a happy nod.

### 3.8 DUSK: "Wick", the lamplighter (v2)

> **This element is an interpretation**, like slinkwing's. The user asked for "a new dragon which is like a dark blue → grey dragon". The reading here takes both senses of the arrow:
>
> - **over its life:** it hatches a deep Prussian blue, keeps its blue through the young and adult stages, and silvers to storm slate as an elder: the cast-wide greying (3.9) made its identity, the steepest in the cast;
> - **along its body:** it is dark blue with grey at the edges (a smoke-grey tail tip, a frosted nose, moth-grey wings), and that grey creeps inward stage by stage.
>
> Its element is **dusk**, the blue hour turning grey; its cue is the light it carries into the evening, a **lamp**. Element id `dusk`; nickname **Wick** (a lamp word, short like Zap; "Wisp", the first draft's, read as thin and frail on an elder and a will-o'-the-wisp is a lure that leads travellers astray: the v2 review). The user is asked to confirm both the element name and the nickname; the runners-up are "Glim" and "Vesper" (the evening star). Built as NB's "Wisp" with NA's grafts and the v2 review's revisions (D24); `elements/dusk.ts` draws all of it since the element pass v2 (its Review log entry), and what follows is as built.

**Identity and care.**
- Gentle, dreamy and sleepy: **the early sleeper** (Echo is the night owl). It makes no sound; its lamp speaks for it (the opposite of Echo, loud and clingy). Drowsy by day, brightest at dusk.
- **Care hook: bedtime.** It wants to be put to bed, and no other element's need is sleep (fire's is food, spike's touch, rock's bond, lightning's play, water's baths, slinkwing's company). A **tuck-in** (petting it once it lies down) turns the lamp down to a nightlight and it sleeps well; with no tuck-in it lies awake with its lamp lit and wakes `grumpy`. A nap takes the gutter out of its lamp. As a baby its lamp is its nightlight. **The tuck-in is an element anim, `tuckin`** (in the family of fire's `bath`; a loop with its intro, ending in the sleep loop): it lies down as it does to sleep but AWAKE, its lamp set on the floor still lit, and waits, eyes lidded, 60 f (× the stage's lie-down); petted (60 to 96) it purrs, `happy`; then its lamp turns down in steps (full, the crescent, the sliver, the new-moon nightlight: 1 / 0.6 / 0.3 / 0 at f 100 / 106 / 112) as its eyes close at 112, and it sleeps from 116. The game's tuck-in plays it and every other element's sleep in its place (anims.ts `ELEMENT_ANIM_FALLBACK`); the refill after a nap is the wake's relight.
- **The lamp's lexicon** (the v2 review: one cue must never carry two care signals that ask for different responses, the rule of rock's two tucks). Each lamp state means one thing:
  - **moon phase and droop = mood**, and only mood (the gauge below);
  - **guttering = tired**, and only tired: a 2 f flicker to the dark face, with the `sleepy` face, more often as bedtime nears (every 240 f, then 120, then 60), gone after a nap (as built it keys off the `sleepy` face, every 60 f: the rig has no tired level yet, the Review log's known gaps);
  - **hunger** is the search by lamplight (4.3): the lamp lowered over where the bowl goes, peered at, no guttering;
  - **out** is the baby's fizzle alone (below), a gag with a "phew";
  - **a new-moon nightlight on the floor = asleep.**
- **The lamp is its own tap target** in the game: a tap on it plays the lamp-bat (the fidget below), so a tap meant for the face or chin never lands on the lamp by accident and the lamp becomes a toy. (Not wired yet: the game has no tap seam; `ElementSpec.reach`, how far the lamp reaches past the snout, is the number for a tap box and for pen spacing.)
- **Social: the habitat's night-light.** Its breath raises `event: 'hush'` (not consumed yet, like `shriek` and `call`), meant to calm neighbours: spike's wary lean eases, slinkwing's lonely call is answered. Echo shrieks in the night; Wick hushes it.
- **Growth, the most visible in the cast.** Every stage-up changes things together: its lamp grows from a bud into a lantern, grey creeps in from its tail tip and nose, its blue drains a little (young, adult) and then silvers (elder). **The lamp never ages:** it is the one colour an elder keeps from hatching.

**The cue: the crook lamp** (zone: ahead of the face; budgets: 3.0). A thin stalk rises from the front of the brow, arches forward and hooks down; a small **lantern** hangs plumb from the hook by a bail, ahead of the snout. Flat black at ÷ 3 it is a crook ahead of the face with a lantern hanging from it. (A round glowing ball on a flesh stalk ahead of the mouth is the anglerfish's lure, and the first draft's 5 × 2 cap on a 9 px disc did not register at 1×, so the lamp is a lantern **shape**, a made thing: the v2 review.)
- **Stalk.** A cubic Bézier in cranium space from a root on the cranium circle (0.5 px inside it) at the skull's top-front, through P1 and P2 to its tip P3 (the table), stroked in `scale`, 2 px (baby, young) or 2.5 px (adult, elder), inside a 1 px ink line each side. It is flesh, so it is flat, and it is drawn at `nearHead` **clipped outside the skull path**, so its ink stops at the skull's contour and stalk and skull are one silhouette (the construction that makes neck and body one: 1.2). Its visible part starts ≥ 1.5 px outside the eye clip and the whole arch lies ahead of the eye.
- **Lantern** (young, adult, elder). A body taller than wide (young 6 × 7, adult and elder 7 × 9), corners rounded 1 px, a `flat()` fill in `glow` inside a 1 px ink ring: an emitter, never banded (D20). On its top a **cap** 2 px tall, a **roof**: its top row as wide as the body, its eave row overhanging the body 1 px each side (cast review v2, the lead's call: flush with the body, cap and finial made a rounded tube with pale ends, a capsule, a pill or a vial at 1×; a square cap read as a battery or a jar), and under it a **finial**, a knob 2 px tall and 2 px narrower each side (young 2 px, adult and elder 3: 3.8's text over the first table's 1 px a side, which matched the cap, so the lantern read the same both ways up, a battery or a capsule), both `horn` (silver), inked with the lantern as one silhouette. It hangs from the stalk's tip by a **bail**, a wire arch of ink 3 px wide with a pixel of background inside it (cast review v2: the first build's 2 px solid `horn` neck showed no loop, and with the moth on the cap's corner the lantern read as a spray bottle), ceil(hook) + bail rows down to the cap, so **2 rows of bail show** on every lantern (the adult's 2.25 px hook left one): the gap is what says "hung" and "made". The whole lantern hangs **plumb in root space** at P3 (counter-rotated like fire's flame, honouring `J.headFlip`) and never sinks below the floor (clamped 1 px above it; the head's floor guard counts it, like the beard). Its swing is **kinematic**, keyed per act: the walk ± 2 px (the baby's ± 3), the growl ± 2, the lamp-bat's swings; it hangs still in the idle (a look-around whip needs the head's angle last frame: not built). **The lantern turns only in the lamp-bat**: every other swing moves it by whole pixels with the bail leaning from the hook to it, and the baby's bud moves its curl's end. The cap turns at most 25°, but the bonk swings it as far as the snout (up to 45°). The **core** is a flat `glow.hi` block: young 2 × 2, adult 3 × 3, elder 3 × 5 (the full moon).
- **Bud** (baby, the seed). A round lamp, r 3 (6 px across), in `glow` in its ink ring, with a flat 4 × 2 lid, a lantern-bud (a round top with a pale highlight read as the anglerfish's lure: cast review v2), hung straight from the hook (no bail: the baby's curl is too short to show one); no core (babies have no highlights).
- **Clearance** (the v2 review: the elder's lowered lamp came within 2 to 3 px of the snout, 1.6 at `mood` −1, and at ÷ 3 the crook closed into a bulb on the nose). ≥ **4 px of background** between the lantern's ink (bail to finial) and the snout's or the jaw's ink at rest and at `mood` −1, young, adult and elder; ≥ 3 px on the baby. Measured in cranium space on the build (below) and re-measured on the rig, with the head's pitch and the pendulum, on the ÷ 3 sheet at `mood` −1. **As built:** the clearance is held by pushing the lamp's end point forward (the stalk's end half as much), never by shortening the stalk, to the stage's clearance + 0.5 px at rest (the adult's measured about 4.5, not 6) and + 1 in the walk, and a swing back toward the face stops at it. The baby's curl runs out level from the brow and drops into the bud, drawn in to the 3 px clearance (cast review v2: hung 5 px further out on a longer curl, the bud on its antenna was a lure). Asleep the stalk bows forward to the nightlight, so it never reads as a trunk.

| Cranium space (+x to the snout, y down) | Baby (seed) | Young (sprout) | Adult (signature) | Elder (the adult's, plus) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Form | a glowing bud on a fiddlehead curl | the curl opens into an arch, a small lantern + a 2 × 2 core | a lantern crook ahead of the nose, + a 3 × 3 core | the adult's crook, unchanged in shape and place, + the full-moon core (3 × 5) and the resident moth (below) |
| Root angle on the cranium; P1; P2; P3 | 60°; (5.5, −11); (12, −12); (16, −10) | 64°; (5.5, −12.5); (18, −13.5); (23.5, −11.5) | 62°; (7, −15); (20.5, −16); (26.5, −13.5) | the adult's |
| Lamp: body; cap; bail; finial | bud r 3; 4 × 2; none; none | 6 × 7; 6 × 2; 2; 2 × 2 | 7 × 9; 7 × 2; 2; 3 × 2 | the adult's |
| Stalk width / length | 2 / 15.6 | 2 / 21.4 | 2.5 / 24.8 | 2.5 / 24.8 |
| Rise above the skull top at rest (at `mood` +1); budget | 4.5 (4.9); ≤ 5 | 5.5 (5.9); ≤ 6 | 7.5 (7.9); ≤ 8 | the adult's |
| Arch peak (x, y) | (12.5, −11.0) | (16.7, −12.5) | (19.0, −14.7) | the adult's |
| Lantern–snout clearance, rest / `mood` −1 (before the head's pitch) | 3.3 / 3.8 | 4.3 / 5.8 | 4.3 / 5.8 | the adult's |

(The stalk moved forward and a little lower than NB's (P3 (14, −8.5) / (19, −10.5) / (25, −12), rise 4.5 / 6.0 / 7.9): the lantern hangs longer than a disc, the soft 0.8 snout sits further back, and the happy lift must stay inside the head budget. The elder keeps the adult's crook: NB's "stooped" elder lamp hung 3.5 px lower, 70 % of its own `mood` −1 droop, so an elder at mood 0 looked unhappy, and a stooped lamplighter with a lantern is the cane read D21 bans: the v2 review.)

- **Mood gauge: the lamp's moon phase and the stalk's droop** (D7). NB measured the stalk and a round lamp's silhouette, idle / `mood` −1 / asleep: adult 169 / 180 / 187 px², young 115 / 119 / 133, baby 89 / 93 / 97; the cue never shrinks, and the arch keeps 69 to 76 % of its rest rise at −1.
